Document 027: 2026-10-27_［門間一夫の経済深読み］成長戦略より21世紀ニッポンの「アポロ計画」を_218ba051

門間 一夫の 
経済深読み

成長戦略より 21 世紀ニッポンの「 ア
ポロ計画」を 
みずほ総合研究所  
エグゼクティブエコノミスト  門間一夫 2026 年 10 月 27 日

心情的にはノーサレンダー路線 
先月の論考では、①度重なる成長戦略にもかかわらず日本の潜在成長率は低下したこと、②成長戦略
には政策手段の特定が困難という基本問題があるこ と 、③ 25 年英知を尽くしても結果が出なかった事実
は厳粛に受け止めるべきこと、などを述べた 1。 
それを踏まえ、本稿ではあるべき方向性を考えてみたい。選択肢は、(a)25 年で諦めず 50 年でも 100
年でもやる、(b)成長目標を達成しやすい水準に下げる、(c)政府のミッションは成長戦略とは別にある
と考える、の 3 つである。選択肢(b)が無難に見えるが、もともと成長戦略は「現状の成長率ではだめ
だ」という認識に基づくものである。現状を明確に上回る成長率を目指さない限り、成長戦略としては
自己矛盾である。 
すると残るのは (a)ノーサレンダー路線、(c)ミッション再考、のいずれかである。このうちノーサレ
ンダー路線、つまり「成長を諦めない」という不屈の姿勢には人々の共感が得られやすい。しかし、不
屈の志を掲げても、手段の裏付けを欠くという現実は変わらない。手段の裏付けを欠くなら「目標」で
はなく「夢」である。もちろん、夢を語ることが悪いわけではないし、政治家は夢を語るべきという考
えもあるだろう。その夢が偶然にでもかなえば歴史に残る偉業であり、夢なら実現せずとも落胆の度合
いは小さい。ノーサレンダー路線はアップサイドだけでダウンサイドはない、と考えてよいだろうか。 
成長戦略のダウンサイド 
成長戦略にダウンサイドはない、やってみて損はない、と い う 前提は実は正しくない。成長戦略は「た
だ」でできるわけではないからだ。例えば、成長戦略には多かれ少なかれネット財政支出が伴う。国債
をどれだけ発行できるのか議論の余地はあるが、いずれにせよ有限である。 
一方、成長戦略は総花的になりやすい。成長を実現する手段がはっきりしない以上、ある政策案を「 成
長とは無関係」ときっぱり排除するのも難しいからである。経済界の要望や政治家の信念に基づく様々
な政策案が、成長に少しでも関係しそうなら幅広く正当化される。各種施策の費用対効果について一定

1 門間一夫の経済深読み「成長戦略の本質的な限界」（2026 年 9 月 25 日 ）。

### Page 2

2

の分析や議論は行われている。しかし、行われることとそれが有効に機能することは別である。費用対
効果の分析が有効に機能し、各種施策の経済成長へのインパクトが正確に測れていたとしたら、25 年に
わたり成長目標の大幅未達という結果にはならなかったはずである。 
一般に、政策目標の実現に向けてリソースが投入され、目標が実現できなかった場合、そのリソース
投入は失敗だったことになる。チャレンジ自体は無駄ではなかったと言える場合もあるが、過去 25 年
だめだった目標にもう一度挑戦してみるという話なら、たぶん次もだめだろうと事前に予想できる。金
も人もふんだんにあるなら別だが、財政状況が厳しく、人材の不足も叫ばれる国で、高確率で失敗する
ミッションへのリソース投入は賢明な選択と言えるのか。 
有限のリソースが無駄に使われれば、他の課題のために使えるリソースが減る。ここで言うリソース
は財政資金だけではない。実現できない目的のために、多忙な官僚の労力が割かれ、政治家のポリティ
カル・キャピタルも消耗すれば、やればできたはずの他の課題への対応まで中途半端に終わってしまう。
成長戦略には他の課題を犠牲にするという「機会費用」が伴うのであり、それが「ただではない」とい
うことのより正確な意味である。「成長を諦めないノーサレンダー路線でいく」という選択は、「50 年で
も 100 年でもその機会費用を払い続けることを覚悟する」という選択でもある。 
何のための経済成長か 
ここで話を一段階前に戻してみよう。そもそもなぜ、経済成長を高めるべきと考えられているのだろ
うか。もちろん、低成長より高成長の方がいいに決まっているので、経済成長を簡単に高められるので
あれば、そんな疑問を持つ必要はない。しかし、経済成長を高めようとすることで機会費用を払い続け
ることになる確率が高いなら、そうまでしてわれわれが最終的に望んでいるものは何なのかという問い
に、きちんと向き合う必要がある。 
経済成長の必要性を訴える主張は、「〇〇の解決には経済成長が不可欠」「低成長のままなら〇〇のよ
うな不都合が起きる」というパターンにおおむね当てはまるように思う。つまり、現在直面している課
題、あるいは将来直面しそうな課題に対応するには、より高い経済成長が必要という認識が成長戦略の
基礎にあると考えられる。念頭に置かれている課題の中身は、社会保障や財政の持続性、行政・医療・
介護サービスの維持、国際的な存在感の回復、生活水準の向上など、論者により様々である。パイを増
やせるなら改善できる課題は多いので、経済成長を高めるべきという主張は万人受けし、政治家には格
好の政策アジェンダになる。 
しかし、「何らかの課題を経済成長で解決する」という考え方は 、「 その課題に直接向き合うより経済
成長を高める方が簡単だ」という前提がないと成立しない。その前提が間違っていれば、むしろ経済成
長に頼るがゆえに肝心の課題がいつまでも解決しないことになる。重要な課題であればあるほど、経済
成長と切り離して解決する努力を早く始めるべきではないか。 
例えば、社会保障や財政の持続性が課題なのであれば、給付と負担のバランスや、恵まれている人と
そうでない人の公平感を改善することで、問題の解決を目指す方が良い。人々の生活水準を高めたいと
いう課題を経済成長と切り離すのは少し厄介だが、国民全員が等しく困っているわけではなく、問題の

### Page 3

3

本質は経済格差にある。分配政策により、国民生活の満足感や社会の安定性を全体として改善すること
はできる。 
国際社会における日本の存在感を高めることは、経済成長ではそもそも無理であろう。かつて日本が
世界第 2 位の GDP をキープし、経済大国として存在感を示した時代もあった。しかし、あれは冷戦構造
のもとでの日米関係、戦後の高度成長とその余熱、新興国が発展初期段階にあったことなどによる歴史
の偶然であった。GDP の対世界シェアが 3～4％程度まで落ち、今後さらに人口が減り、資源・エネルギ
ーに乏しく、特殊な言語を使う島国が、今から経済成長を多少高められたとしても、そのことで示せる
国際パワーは高が知れている。小さくても存在感がある国をどう作るかが、正しいアジェンダ設定だと
思われる。 
21 世紀ニッポンの「アポロ計画」は？ 
日本が課題だらけなのは確かだが、25 年間どうやっても達成できなかった経済成長に、その解決をこ
れ以上頼るのが正解とは思いにくい。逆に、優先課題の解決に国が本気でリソースを投入するなら、そ
こに民間も収益機会を見出し、様々な連鎖反応を通じて経済に好影響が及ぶ可能性も出てくる。 
経済学者マリアナ・マッツカートは著書「ミッション・エコノミー」において、大きな変革は市場メ
カニズムだけでは生み出せず、国家が野心的だが実行可能な課題を設定し、民間の力を引き出す仕組み
を築くことが重要と説く 2。マッツカートは代表例として、1960 年代の米国アポロ計画を挙げる。1969
年 7 月 20 日、ニール・アームストロングとバズ・オルドリンが月面に降り立ったことで知られる、あの
アポロ計画である。 
日本の成長戦略も「野心的な課題」を掲げたものではあるが、複雑すぎるマクロ経済そのものを課題
の対象にすると、先月の論考で詳しく述べたように、目的と手段の関係性が特定できない。原因と結果
の関係がよくわからない仕事は、責任の所在があいまいで互いに人任せになり、結果にコミットするこ
との効果が得られない。それに対し、「月に人を送る」などのより具体的なミッションの場合、そのため
には何が必要なのかの因果関係を分析・認識しやすい分、結果にコミットすれば人々の力が正しい方向
へ向かう。 
アポロ計画は、半導体、コンピューター、通信、材料工学などの技術を飛躍的に発展させ、その後の
米国の産業基盤を築いたと言われている。また、計画推進の国家機関であった米航空宇宙局（NASA）は 、
多くの科学技術人材を育成・輩出した。彼らはその後、金融イノベーションなどでも活躍し、NASA 出身
なので「ロケット・サイエンティスト」と呼ばれたことはよく知られている。 
ここで重要なのは、半導体やコンピューターなどの産業育成自体を目的として、政府が補助金で支援
したという話ではないことである。アポロ計画という具体的なミッションがまず存在し、それへの貢献
に適正な対価で報いる仕組みがイノベーターたちを突き動かし、様々な技術や知見が開発、蓄積された
のである。後世に残った産業基盤や人材は、「月に人を送る」というミッションの副産物であった。

2 Mazzucato, Mariana(2021) Mission Economy: A Moonshot Guide to Changing Capitalism, Allen Lane-Penguin 
（関美和・鈴木絵里子訳『ミッション・エコノミー』News Picks、2021 年 ）。

### Page 4

4

一般論として、何らかの課題を解決すれば、経済に必ず大きなプラスの効果が見込める、とまでは言
えない。しかし、上位目標が「課題の解決」なのだから、それが達成された時点でミッションは成功で
あり、投入されたリソースは「有効に使われた」ことになる。そのうえで、マクロ経済にもプラスの波
及効果があればなお良いが、仮にそれがなくても課題が解決された分、以前より望ましい社会になって
いる。 
最大の難関は、優先課題を特定しそれに取り組む社会的モメンタムを生み出すことであり、それには
既存の発想に捉われない強力なビジョンとリーダーシップが要る。アポロ計画にも当初「無謀で財政資
金の無駄使い」という声があった。マッツカートは先ほどの著書の中で、仮に現代風の費用対効果分析
を適用していたら、アポロ計画は採用されず、人類が月面に立つことはなかったと言っている。 
アポロ計画に相当する案件は、どの時代、どの国にも自明なものとしてあるわけではない。「月に人を
送る」というミッションは、当時の米ソ冷戦状況において、ソ連に宇宙開発で打ち勝つことが米国の死
活問題、という歴史特殊的な認識のもとで生まれた。それでも、よく考えてみれば今の日本にも、人口
の減少・高齢化や地政学環境の激変など、人類史上にもまれな歴史特殊的な条件のもとで、「死活問題」
になりつつある課題はいくつかあるように思う。例えば、エネルギーや食料の安定供給、生活インフラ・
医療・介護体制の維持・改善などは、次世代の日本人にとってまさに「死活問題」なのではないか。 
限られたリソースの優先課題への配分が、市場メカニズムで実現できないなら、国が強力に関与しな
ければならない。民間には不得意な国民的課題の解決こそ、国の本来の役割であり、そのためのリソー
スを国債発行でまかないきれないなら、国民に追加負担を求めてもいい。 
言うまでもなくこれは困難な作業である。だから優先課題は何か、そのために国民負担を求めるべき
かなどを深く議論せず、経済成長で様々な問題をまとめて解決できることにしておく方が、実はずっと
楽なのである。しかし、それが問題の先送りにすぎないことは繰り返し述べたとおりである。今も成長
戦略が重要だと言い、政府にそれを求めたがるわれわれの意識は、日本が直面する真の課題やその優先
順位を考えるのは面倒だ、という思考停止から生まれているのかもしれない。21 世紀ニッポンの「アポ
ロ計画」は何なのか、国民的な議論の活性化が必要ではないだろうか。

アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大 
供給制約への対応力が投資の持続性を左右

○ アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大している。その背景には、AI・クラウド需要の拡大や各国・
地域の投資誘致策、クラウド事業者の戦略、建設コストの低さなどが挙げられる 
○ データセンター投資が増加することは、総固定資本形成の増加や、半導体・サーバーなどの輸出
拡大、雇用創出、技術・知識の移転につながる可能性がある 
○ 今後も需要拡大が見込まれる一方、立地地域が抱える電力・水などのインフラ負荷は大きい。 
投資の経済効果と負荷とのバランスを踏まえた政策対応が、投資拡大の持続性を左右するだろう 
１．アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大 
アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大している。図表1は、同地域におけるデータセンター関連の対内
直接投資計画額を示したものである。データセンター投資は2024年頃から大きく増加し、2026年上半
期も高水準を維持している。主要な投資先であるASEAN向け投資が一段と拡大したほか、2025年以降は
インド、オーストラリアでも投資が急増している。 
また、アジアのFDI流入におけるデータセンターの存在感も高まっている。同地域の対内直接投資計
画額全体に占めるデータセンターの割合は、2025年に約30％まで上昇した（図表2）。

２．アジアにおいてデータセンター投資がなぜ増加しているのか 
アジアでデータセンター投資が拡大している背景には、投資受入国におけるデジタル関連需要の増
加や、それを受けた投資誘致の活発化、また投資側企業にとってコスト面などから当該地域の相対的
な優位性が増していることが挙げられる。 
実際、投資受入国ではデジタルサービスに対する需要が拡大している。Gelvanovska-Garcia et al.
（2024）によれば、東アジア・太平洋地域では、インターネットの普及や経済のデジタル化を背景に、
製造業や医療など幅広い分野でクラウドサービスの導入が進んでいると指摘している。こうしたサー
ビスの利用拡大は、データの処理・保存を担うデータセンターの需要を高めることにつながる。 
また、各国政府がデータセンター誘致を活発化させていることも、アジアにおけるデータセンター
投資を後押ししている。図表3は、各国のデータセンター関連の優遇策を示したものだが、各国で誘致
を目的とした優遇政策がとられている。タイやインドでは、一定の条件を満たすデータセンター事業
や関連事業者に対して、所得税等の免税措置にまで踏み込んでいる。 
対して、投資側企業にとっては、需要地の近くにデータセンターを整備することによる、自社サー
ビスの品質向上が期待される1。事実、アジアにおける主要なデータセンター投資案件の企業発表にお
いても、アジアへの投資目的として、現地企業や政府への低遅延サービス提供、通信環境の強靭化な
どが挙げられている2。 
サービス品質向上に加え、ASEANやインド等における建設コストの低さも投資を後押しする要因とな
っている。図表4は、データセンターにおけるIT負荷容量1メガワットあたりの建設コストを比較した
ものだが、インドやベトナム、インドネシアなどはデータセンターの建設費用が相対的に小さく、シ
ンガポール等の地域に比べ、低コストでデータセンターの容量増強が可能である。

図表 3 データセンター誘致策の例 図表 4 データセンターの建設コスト

トは、中程度の設備仕様を前提とした推計値を使用。サ

３．データセンター投資はアジア経済に何をもたらすのか 
データセンター投資の拡大は、アジア経済にどのような影響をもたらすのだろうか。主なポイント
は以下の4つである。 
まず第一に、総固定資本形成の押し上げである。データセンターの設置時、企業は建物を建設する
ほか、サーバーや受変電、冷却、通信等の設備を購入する。こうした企業活動は、GDP統計上では総固
定資本形成中の建設・機械設備投資として計上され、経済成長率の押し上げ要因となる3。実際、オー
ストラリアでは2025年以降、情報・通信部門の機械投資がGDP成長率を押し上げており、データセンタ
ー投資拡大の影響がうかがえる。 
また第二のポイントは、AI関連財の輸出拡大である。東アジアやASEANは、半導体やサーバー等の製
造拠点であり、製品需要の拡大は輸出の追い風となる。図表5に示す通り、アジアのAI関連財輸出はAI
ブームのもとで急拡大が続いている。とりわけ、2026年以降は中国などアジア域内向け輸出の増勢が
顕著であり、アジア域内におけるデータセンター設置拡大の影響がみてとれる。 
そして第三のポイントは、雇用の拡大である。データセンターの設置時に建設や設備設置の作業員
が必要となるほか、稼働後も設備管理やサーバー運用に関わる人材の雇用が創出される可能性がある。 
最後は、技術・知識の波及である。データセンターを運営する外国企業が現地企業と取引すること
により、外国企業から現地企業へ技術が伝わることが期待される。例えば、電力・冷却・通信設備の
設計や施工、保守管理にかかるノウハウを、現地企業が獲得するケースなどがある。 
ただし、経済波及効果の実現には不確実性が伴う点は留意する必要がある。中でも、雇用への波及
効果は限定的なものに留まる可能性がある。というのも、fDi Marketsが集計する投資計画データによ
ると（図表6）、海外直接投資100万米ドルあたりの直接雇用創出数は、データセンター投資の場合、全
産業平均よりも少数にとどまるからだ。 
図表 5 アジアのAI関連財輸出額（仕向け地別） 図表 6 海外直接投資100万米ドルあたりの

４．データセンター投資の先行きとリスク要因 
今後のデータセンター投資は、需要面での拡大余地が大きいと見込まれる一方、供給面では様々な
制約が表面化する可能性がある。 
まず、需要面については、クラウド需要の拡大がデータセンターの投資を促す公算が大きい。実際、
主要な民間調査会社の予想によれば、電子商取引やAI利活用の浸透によりデータの保存・処理需要が
増加し、アジアにおけるクラウド市場の拡大が続くとされている4。 
一方、先行きの供給制約となりうる要因も少なくない。具体的には、電力・水などの資源制約と、
政策変更の2つである。 
資源制約については、第一に電力供給の制約がある。アジアでは、今後も電力需要の拡大が見込ま
れる一方で、それに見合った電力供給の拡大が容易ではないからだ。図表7は、IEAが2026年2月に公表
した、2026～2030年の年平均増加率の予測を示している。掲載した各国・地域ではいずれも電力需要
の増加が見込まれ、特にインドやベトナムなどで高い伸びとなっていることがわかる。産業活動の拡
大や冷房需要の増加、データセンターの集積などが需要拡大をけん引する格好だ。 
一方、電力供給の伸びは、需要と比べ、緩やかなものにとどまる可能性が高い。IEAによれば、東南
アジアの電力生産量は2026～2030年に年平均5.2％の増加と、電力需要の伸び（年平均5.4％）を下回
り、電力需給のひっ迫が続くことが想定されている5。 
また、データセンターの立地が特定地域に集中しやすい点にも留意が必要だ6。特定地域で電力需給
がひっ迫した場合、送電網の不足などで他地域からの融通ができない場合がある7。 
資源制約については、電力のみならず、水の安定供給という課題もある。冷却に大量の水を使用す
るデータセンターでは、施設の新設・拡張に伴って水需要が増加すると見込まれるからだ。必要な水
量を確保できなければ、施設の稼働が制約されることになりかねない。ASEAN事務局（2025）は、デー
タセンターの冷却に伴う水需要の増加は、農業・工業・住民生活向けの水利用と競合する可能性があ
ると指摘している8。 
二点目は、政策変更のリスクである。雇用拡大等
を通じた地場経済への貢献度の低さや、資源利用や
安全性をめぐる地域住民からの反発などを背景に、
投資許認可の厳格化や新規開発の制限が行われる
可能性がある。 
地場経済への貢献については、先述の通り、デー
タセンターの直接的な雇用創出効果は他の投資に
比べて限られる。その結果、受入国・地域が期待し
ていた経済効果よりも現地経済への貢献が小さい
となった場合、投資誘致や優遇税制の見直しにつな
がる可能性は否定できない。また、地域住民からの
反発については、図表8で示す通り、既にマレーシ
ア、インドネシア、インド、オーストラリアなどで、
水利用や生活環境への影響などを巡り、データセン

5

ター投資への抗議や反対運動が起きている。 
こうした状況のもと、一部の国ではすでにデータセンター投資関連の規制を見直す動きが広がって
いる。実際にタイでは、政府による委員会組織（データセンタービジネス委員会）が、データセンタ
ー166件の建設や許可手続きを一時停止した。停止の理由としては、資源の利用、立地、安全性に関す
る全国的な基準の策定が必要であることが挙げられている9。タイ政府は投資誘致を維持すると示して
いるものの、予見可能性の低下から、進捗中案件の見直しや計画中案件の中止につながるリスクは完
全に拭えない。

５．供給制約への対応力がデータセンター投資の行方を左右 
アジアでは、AI・クラウド需要の拡大を背景に、今後もデータセンター投資の増加が続くと見込ま
れる。ただし、巨額の投資に比べて雇用創出効果は限定的とみられる一方、電力・水などのインフラ
負荷は大きく、投資の拡大が必ずしも受入国・地域に十分な経済的便益をもたらすとは限らない。 
こうした中、今後のデータセンター投資では、建設コストの低さや税制優遇に加え、電力・水の安
定供給や規制・許認可の予見可能性などが、投資先を選別する上で一段と重要になると考えられる。
供給制約が深刻化する地域では、投資計画の遅延や縮小が生じる一方、安定的な事業環境を確保でき
る地域に投資が集中する可能性もある。 
受入国にとっても、単に投資額の拡大を追求するのではなく、国内産業への波及やインフラ整備の
負担を見極めることが重要となる。今後のアジアにおけるデータセンター投資は、需要拡大が成長を
支える一方で、供給制約への対応力の違いが、各国・地域のへの投資配分を左右することになるだろ
う。

図表 8 データセンターを巡る抗議活動の例

延が減少し、データ送受信にかかる時間が短期化すると指摘されている。 
2 fDi Markets や生成 AI による情報収集により、アジアにおける主要なデータセンター投資案件に関する企業発表の内容を確認
した。マレーシア、台湾等の投資案件において、通信速度に関する内容がみられた。 
3 サーバーや関連設備を海外から輸入する場合、輸入増加が GDP を押し下げる方向に働く部分もあるため、投資額の全てが国内
付加価値の増加につながるわけではない。 
4 CBRE、McKinsey & Company 等の民間調査会社のレポートを参照した。 
5IEA（2026）“ASEAN Energy Security Review”では、東南アジアの電力需要は 2026～2030 年に年平均 5.4％増加すると予測さ
れている。電力生産量の伸びは、IEA（2026）“Electricity 2026”、 IEA（2026）“ASEAN Energy Security Review”に基づき、
IEA の 2024 年実績値と 2025 年の前年比伸び率、2030 年の予測値を基準に、年平均成長率が一定となるよう補間し計算したも
の。 
6 IEA（2025）“Energy and AI”は、データセンターは電力負荷が大きく、地理的にも集中する傾向があるため、発電能力や送
電網容量を大幅に増強する必要が生じる可能性があること、また電力供給が途切れないよう安定的な電力網を構築する必要があ
ることを指摘している。 
7 ベトナム北部では、2023 年 5～6 月に断続的な停電が発生し、多くの家庭や企業に影響が広がった。World Bank（2023）
“Taking Stock, August 2023: Making Public Investment Work for Growth”は、送電網整備の遅れにより、南部の余剰発電
能力を北部の電力供給に十分に活用できなかったことが停電の一因となったと指摘している。 
8 ASEAN（2025）“ASEAN Guide for Sustainable Data Centre Development”によれば、データセンターの中でもハイパースケ
ール施設の冷却システムでは、水使用量が 1 日当たり最大 150 万リットルに達する場合があり、干ばつが発生しやすい地域の水
供給や生態系に大きな負荷を与えうると説明している。また、水利用の効率化を促す政策的枠組みがなければ、データセンター
の水需要が、農業・工業・地域住民に必要な水の確保を圧迫する可能性を指摘している。 
9 「DC 開発 166 件を一時停止、全国的な基準策定へ」『NNA ASIA』、2026 年 9 月 7 日。

Nagel)亦表示通膨前景尚未改善，歐洲央行應於 12 月升息。多位歐洲央行官員

溫，預計將支撐歐元走勢。

佳，預計將為美元提供支撐。

業率亦由前月的 4.2%降至 4.1%，顯示勞動市場仍具韌性，在美國經濟穩健及通
膨維持高位的情況下，強化聯準會 12月升息預期。
 澳洲央行於 11/3 將現金基準利率調升一碼至 4.85%，係今年以來第五次升息並
創下 2008 年 11 月以來新高水準。然而，此次九名委員中有三人主張維持利率
不變，投票結果出現分歧，加上貨幣政策委員會聲明中，刪除有關可能進一步升
息的明確表述，令市場預期澳洲央行將於 12月暫停升息。

貶值 2 角 9 分。 
    上週台幣走貶，週初，台股開低，市場對 AI投資支出及科技股估值的疑慮，致
使外資連續多日賣超台股並匯出，台幣連貶數日，加以週間美國期中選舉結果出爐，
民主黨勝選，市場擔憂 AI產業監管趨嚴，台股重挫，外資上週累計賣超台股 5,244
億，外資強勢匯出之下，儘管逢高有出口商拋匯抑制台幣貶值速度，台幣仍貶破 33
整數大關，一度觸及年內低點 33.040，惟週五午盤過後，外資及壽險轉向匯入，加
以出口商拋匯需求，台幣跌幅收斂，終場在央行調節之下收在 32.768。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。就國際市場而言，美國非農就業數據意外高於預期、
通膨壓力仍在，強化市場對於聯準會 12月升息預期，高利率將有助國際美元走升；
就台灣市場來看，市場對 AI產業資本支出、融資成本及信用風險產生疑慮，加以民
主黨勝選，引發市場對 AI監管政策收緊與資料中心建設放緩的擔憂，外資持續賣超
台股，令台幣持續面臨貶值的壓力，惟出口商拋匯需求待消化，將抑制台幣貶值速
度，綜合以上，預估本週台幣走勢偏弱。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：32.600-33.200

美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Nov/09/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣先升後貶。週初，國際美元偏弱震盪，人民幣受益走升，境內人民
幣相隔四年多首次升破 6.65整數大關；惟週二中國央行宣布調升外匯存款準備金率，
顯示其抑制單邊升值預期的意圖，人民幣大幅走貶；週間，市場持續消化人行政策
影響，人民幣走貶，最低一度貶至 6.6855，不過美國期中選舉結果出爐，市場不確
定性下降，國際油價、美債殖利率及美元指數皆下跌，緩解人民幣的賣壓，惟中國
國有大行逢低持續買進美元，人民幣反彈力道有限，終場境外人民幣收在 6.6736，
週線貶值 212 點，境內人民幣則收在 6.6763，週線貶值 203 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
     預估本週人民幣區間震盪。就國際市場來看，美國非農就業人數新增 16.5 萬
人，就業率下降至 4.1%，勞動力市場意外強勁，強化市場對於聯準會 12 月再次升
息的預期，利差因素支撐美元指數反彈，將抑制人民幣走升；就中國國內市場來看，
人行最新政策顯示其抑制人民幣單邊升值的意圖，惟第四季為結匯旺季，出口商實
質拋匯需求部分緩解人民幣貶值壓力；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，
USD/CNH重返五日均線之上，顯示人民幣強勢升值的趨勢暫歇，綜合以上，預估本
週人民幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6600-6.6900

美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Nov/09/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週日圓走弱。週初，市場等待美國期中選舉結果，交投偏謹慎觀望，USD/JPY
在 158 附近波動；週間，美國期中選舉由民主黨取得參眾議院多數席次，政治僵局
擔憂令美元走弱，USD/JPY最低來到 157.39；臨近週末，美國公布就業數據優於市
場預期，USD/JPY止跌回升，終場收在 158.45，週線上漲 100 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 USD/JPY區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、日本央行於季度展望報告中
表示，日本近期實際利率上升，基礎通膨率已加速升至 2%附近，加上日本央行近期
亦強調政策已進入避免通膨超標為重點的新階段，進一步強化了其鷹派立場，市場
預期日本最快將於 12月再次升息，預計將支撐日圓走升。二、美國 10月 ISM製造
業 PMI 為 55.5，優於市場預期，顯示製造業擴張速度加快；另外，美國公布 10 月
非農就業人數增加 16.5 萬人，優於市場預期的 6.5 萬人，美國經濟強勁、勞動市場
仍具韌性，強化聯準會 12月升息預期，美元持穩，預計將有利 USD/JPY走勢。三、
日本財務大臣片山皋月重申美日聯合匯率聲明仍具效力，將於必要時採取外匯干預
行動，以維護外匯市場有序運作，將限縮 USD/JPY上行空間。四、就技術面而言，
在日線圖中，USD/JPY短中天期均線趨平，近期大致在 157 至 158.50 間盤整，匯價
暫無明確方向性。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY本週呈現區間震盪的可能性較高。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：157.00-159.50

美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Nov/09/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 先上後下。週初，在美伊傳出有望和談消息後，歐元自低點小
幅走升至 1.1237；週間，美國民主黨於期中選舉大獲全勝，川普選舉失利，美元偏
弱震盪；臨近週末，美國公布非農就業人數高於市場預期，歐元自高點回落，終場
收在 1.1143，週線下跌 12點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD震盪偏弱，主要原因如下：一、歐元區 10月 CPI年增率自
前月的 3.8%上升至 4%，創下三年來的新高，通膨面臨上行風險。此外，歐洲央行
管委暨斯洛伐克央行總裁卡日米爾(Peter Kazimir)表示，歐洲央行將持續升息，以防
止高通膨根深蒂固，並表示目前主要考量已非是否需採取進一步行動，而是須衡量
應升息幾次方能將通膨拉回 2%目標水準，市場預期歐洲央行未來一年內將再升息
二至三次，升息預期升溫，預計將支撐歐元走勢。二、美國公布 10月非農就業人數
增加 16.5 萬人，優於市場預期的 6.5 萬人，另 10 月失業率亦由前月的 4.2%降至
4.1%，顯示勞動市場仍具韌性，強化了聯準會 12月升息預期，美元再度走升至 102，
預計將限縮歐元走升空間。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD各天期均線向
下發散，近期匯價多次向上測試 1.1270 均拉回留下長上影線，顯示上方仍有賣壓待
消化。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD本週呈現震盪偏弱的可能性較高。

一、 台幣貨幣市場部分，受台股重挫影響，令週五資金需求上揚，但提存期初市場
資金充裕，短率持穩於近期區間。 
週二央行標售 364天期定存單 1100億元，加權平均得標利率為 1.577%，低於
市場預期區間 1.59%-1.61%，較上次標售結果下跌 2.5個基本點。 
週五隔夜拆款利率區間在 0.805%~0.825%， 30 天期短票主要成交在
1.67%~1.69%，利率較上週上揚 1 個基本點。 
二、 本週市場交投維持冷清，10 年期指標券 A15109R及 5年期指標券 A15108均
未成交，週五等殖系統參考報價中價分別為 1.885%、1.725%。 
股市承壓帶動避險需求，美債利率下滑，令先前台債偏空氛圍緩解，週三標售
30年期公債得標利率 2.267%，低於市場預期區間 2.27%~2.30%，投標倍數 1.85
倍，較上次 1.55 倍提升，不過考量台灣年底仍有升息可能，將抑制買盤向下
追價意願，預估台債利率區間盤整，預估 5 年券區間為 1.70%~1.75%，10 年
券區間為 1.85%~1.90%。

Cloud Semis:  Peak Growth, Less
Upside
We turn cautious on cloud semis as our US team expects ~20%

of 2028 US data center capacity additions could shift to 2029,
potentially impacting cloud semis suppliers' 2028 earnings.
What’s Changed

2028 capex under the spotlight. We turned neutral on cloud semi suppliers in

September 2024 on capex/revision peaking risks and GB200 execution concerns,
then upgraded Aspeed in June 2025 as revisions approached a trough and rack-level
execution improved (Cloud semis: time to turn constructive). AI agents reinforced
our conviction in inference demand.  In our recent note, Cloud Semis: Growing Cloud
Capex into 2027, we remained constructive  on our Cloud Semis coverage due to
ongoing hyperscaler capex growth.  However, after the US midterms, we are mindful

of delays in project approvals and initiation, and existing labor and material
constraints, in terms of what that could mean for hyperscalers' 2028 capex, and the
build-out and capex cycle.   Therefore, we see rising risks  over the next 2-4 quarters
for our coverage; earnings growth should provide support, but further multiple
expansion may become more difficult, as the direction of growth matters more,
even as absolute growth remains strong.
Cut Montage & Aspeed to EW. From our Asia supply-chain perspective, peaking

revision breadth and greater uncertainty on 2027/28 general-server production
warrants more conservative forecasts. Aspeed: we continue to like the stock from a
longer-term perspective given its strong supply chain positioning but see limited
rerating potential. Montage: we see potentially slower China procurement and
derating risk; structural content growth remains supportive, but exposure reduction
is preferable before deceleration. Upside risks include a resumption of earnings

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Revisions look mature: Earnings-revision breadth is peaking; Asia supply-chain
visibility on 2027/28 general-server production is weakening.
Reduce exposure: Lower Montage estimates; downgrade Aspeed/Montage to EW.
November 9, 2026 04:00 PM GMT

### Page 2

M
Idea
2
The cloud capex cycle is being impacted by the US midterms: Our US team now expects a ~20% of 2028 US data center capacity
additions to shift to 2029,  including some projects in Texas and Ohio, as governors introduce new data center legislation (Buy the Drop: AI
Investment Opportunities Post the US Midterms by Stephen Byrd). Stephen has shifted ~5GW of US data center capacity additions from
2028 to 2029  ( Exhibit 1 ) (equivalent to 20% of US and 10% of global 2028 capacity additions). This implies US data center GW additions

decline 4% Y/Y in 2028 before rising 38% Y/Y in 2029, while global data center additions increase 9% and    20% Y/Y in 2028 and 2029.
Our US team believes this could create an air pocket across the tech supply chain in 2028, potentially reducing pricing power and leading
to a inventory build. Despite potential project delays, our US team maintains its  hyperscaler capex forecasts (Buy the Drop: AI Investment
Opportunities Post the US Midterms) at US$1.6trn/US$1.8trn in 2027/28 ( Exhibit 4 ), as it  expects the hyperscalers to spend and build as

much capacity as they can, while attempting to adjust their build locations to other states that are more data center friendly (like
Tennessee, Louisiana, and others).

Aspeed: Valuation Methodology
We cut our price target (base case scenario value) from   NT$24,000 to NT$14,417: This
primarily reflects lower growth assumptions, alongside earnings revisions. We reduce our
terminal growth rate from 5.5% to 3.0% to better align with macro growth outlook, and
reduce our intermediate growth rate from 21.4% to 18.0% to reflect a potential slowdown
in market demand.   Other key assumptions are unchanged, including:

• Cash payout ratio of 85%
• Cost of equity of 9.8% (2% risk-free rate, 6% risk premium, 1.3 beta)
Our bull and bear case scenario values  also decrease from NT$29,300 and NT$11,500, to
NT$17,600 and NT$6,900, respectively, implying 51x/20x our 2027e EPS estimates.

Montage: Valuation Methodology
We cut our A-share price target to Rmb180 from Rmb377: The change reflects our
lower earnings estimates. Our price target is our base case value, derived from a residual
income (RI) model, which we believe best captures the company's sustainable long-term
growth.  We also cut our intermediate growth rate estimate from 19.3% to 13.6% to reflect
weaker downstream demand estimates. Other RI model assumptions are unchanged,

including a 8.4% cost of equity (beta 1.2, risk-free rate 3.0%, and risk premium 4.5%), a
payout ratio of 30%, and a terminal growth rate of 4.0%. We apply an updated exchange
rate of HK$1.15 : Rmb1, assuming no H-share premium or discount to the A-share, to derive
our H-share price target, which we lower to HK$207 from HK$432.
For the A-share, we cut our bull case value to Rmb218 from Rmb472, and our bear case
value to Rmb83 from Rmb171. For the H-share, we cut our bull case value to HK$259 from

HK$540, and our bear case value to HK$94 from HK$196. These revisions are in line with
our base case value changes.

MS Rating
▪ Montage is China’s only pure datacenter
player, with the majority of its revenue
generated from interconnect chips, mainly
memory interconnect chips for server
DRAM.
▪ Core DRAM interconnect business is
levered to the global cloud capex/memory

from AI server demand.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

We hosted NYPCB’s 3Q26 post result on November 9th. Key 
takeaways include: 
(1) NYPCB mentioned its 4Q26 GM on ABF substrate already 
exceeds 40% (vs. ~15% in 2025 and 25% in 1H26) driven mainly by 
the solid high-end product demand (which contributed 50%+ of the 
ABF revenue in 2026 vs. <20% in 2025), while the company 
mentioned it will gain more high-end market share in 2027 which 
should further drive company’s overall GM level.  
(2) For the new high-end projects in 2027, NYPCB mentioned it will 
gain significant share in the key US CSP next-gen AI project, and 
high-end server CPU project. Also, the company mentioned it 
expects a proactive adoption on 1.6T Ethernet switch demand 
driven by CSPs investment in AI infrastructure, driving its high-end 
switch substrate ASP (the upgrade on substrate, from 800G to 1.6T, 
suggesting a 50%+ higher substrate unit price, per GSe).  
(3) In terms of the ABF substrate pricing, NYPCB believes its ABF 
substrate ASP will further go up by 10-15% QoQ in 4Q26, while it 
also expects the pricing hike ratio to be at least maintained at this 
level from 1Q27-4Q27, without seeing much downside risk. 
Moreover, the company believes the ASP growth could be even 
faster than its expectation if the high-end orders accelerate in 
coming quarters or if they see an even tigher S/D outlook for the 
overall ABF substrate industry.  
(4) In terms of the BT substrate pricing outlook, the company 
mentioned the pricing hike ratio will be low single digits % QoQ in 
4Q26, which will further trend up in 1Q27-4Q27, with a high 
confidence level on the shortage of BT substrate in coming quarters.  
(4) In terms of 4Q26 revenue guidance, the company is expecting 
~20% QoQ revenue growth, driven mainly by the high-end ABF 
projects ramp up + new AI project ramps + BT substrate pricing hike

will not do any equity based fundraising, but will rely on internal cash or bank loan.  
(6) NYPCB also mentioned it will focus only on CoPoS technology, whose substrate 
pricing is also US$1000+ (vs. US$100-200 for the most high end ABF substrate now), 
while the product demand could start ramping up from 2028 and start contributing 
significant revenue from 2029. For EMIB-T, NYPCB believes the penetration rate could 
only be 10% in the foreseeable future, and it has no plan to invest in this small market.  
We maintain our positive view on the overall ABF substrate industry, with 34%/51% 
shortage ratio in 2027/28 (see here), and believe such high shortage ratio will protect 
companies’ GM/OPM even if we start to see some slowdown on AI CAPEX in coming 
years. We started to see all ABF substrate players’ GM surpass 40% in 4Q26, which is 
much faster than our original expectation (Taiwan ABF substrate suppliers reach 40%+ 
GM at the 6-7th quarters of the shortage in last upcycle, but it only takes 3-4 quarters 
for Taiwan players to hit the 40%+ GM, driven mainly by the solid AI upgrading demand 
this time). We expect NYPCB’s ABF substrate GM could reach historical peak level 
(55-60%) in 2H27, suggesting a fruitful upside for the EPS growth in coming years (we 
expect NYPCB’s EPS to grow by 150%+ 2025-29E CAGR), and believe the ABF substrate 
will continue to be a key bottleneck of the overall semi/tech supply chain in the next 3-5 
years.  
Maintain Buy on NYPCB, as the key spot price ABF substrate supplier in the market 
which should benefit strongly from the S/D shortage and pricing hike in the 
foreseeable future, with an unchanged TP of NT$2,700 (based on 21x 2028E EPS).  
Investment Thesis & PT methodology and risks

Investment Thesis - NYPCB 
NYPCB manufactures and markets printed circuit boards (PCBs) and integrated circuit 
(IC) substrates (including ABF and BT substrates). We are Buy rated on NYPCB 
considering the company’s high exposure to BT substrates and non-LTA ABF substrate 
(70%+ of total revenue), where we expect GM/OPM to increase significantly in the 
coming quarters (from breakeven in OPM in 1H25 to 26/42% in 2026/27E) due to the 
favorable substrate pricing outlook. Despite the company’s low exposure to high-end 
ABF substrate industry (<20% of total revenue in 2025), we believe the expanding 
demand from ASIC AI server and high-end switch IC demand will drive the company’s 
high-end ABF revenue exposure to 40%+ by 2027E, suggesting better GM/OPM outlook 
in the long term. In 2H25-2H26, we believe NYPCB will continue to benefit from stronger 
ABF/BT pricing and should be a key beneficiary of the pricing uptrend considering the 
company’s key focus on BT and non-LTA ABF substrate industries. Moreover, in the long 
term (after 2026), we expect the ABF industry to return to supply tightness, and NYPCB’s 
expanding high-end exposure will lead to a long-term earnings growth outlook (148% 
2026-28E earnings CAGR). As a result, we see solid upside potential for NYPCB’s

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As expected, the RBA Monetary Policy Board (MPB) lifted
the cash rate 25bps to 4.85% at its November meeting.
This follows the increase at the previous (late-September)
meeting and is a marked turnaround from the outlook back
in August. The post-meeting media statement cited higher

global energy prices resulting from the Middle East conflict,
tighter domestic capacity pressures and an increase in
inflation expectations for the decision.
However, the decision was a split vote, 6 votes to 3, a
possibility we have flagged for some time. In the post-meeting
media conference, Governor Bullock explained that the
dissenting minority put more weight on the downside risks and
did not see signs of wages growth picking up as much as the

staff forecasts implied. Those members were reportedly more
confident that slower growth would prevent higher inflation
expectations becoming entrenched.
Financial conditions are restrictive and this is expected to
bring inflation down over time. Because the RBA assesses the
risks to the inflation outlook to still be skewed to the upside,
the outlook for coming meetings will still be for a decision
between holding and hiking. But we note that the post-meeting

statement omitted September’s language “including increasing
the cash rate target further if needed” from the commitment to
do what is necessary to get inflation down.
In the post-meeting media conference, the Governor
commented that after five hikes, this is probably a good time
to “take stock”. Together with the dissents, we read this as
confirming our current view that the MPB will keep rates
on hold in December. There is also considerable data flow

between now and then, which could go either way, including
the Q3 national accounts.
Similarly, a lot could happen between now and February, but
we see it as the next most likely timing for a hike, should one
eventuate. We still see that as a risk rather than a base case.
Revised forecasts published in the accompanying Statement
on Monetary Policy (SMP) show underlying inflation lingering
around current rates for longer. This contrasts with the
previous SMP forecasts in August, which showed trimmed

mean inflation returning to the 2–3%yr target range by late
2027 and a little below the 2.5%yr target midpoint in 2028.
We note, however, that the conditioning assumptions on oil
prices, which are based on futures prices, were at a local peak
at the time the forecasts were finalised.
RBA hikes cash rate to new high of 4.85%, a December pause likely
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The RBA’s GDP growth forecasts for 2027 and 2028 were
both revised down –0.3%pts to 1.3% for 2027 and 1.5% in

2028, despite an upward revision to the near term. This is
well below even the RBA’s downbeat view of potential output
growth. Within the GDP aggregate, recent misses and leading
indicators have required upward revisions to the near-term
outlooks for business and dwelling investment, and downside
revisions to public demand. These revisions were offset further
out with downward revisions to the 2028 outlook for business
and dwelling investment and an upward revision to public

demand. Household consumption forecasts were revised down
across most of the profile.
With the unemployment rate already at 4.7% in August and
September – a rate the RBA did not forecast until end-2027 in
its August SMP forecasts – the RBA has lifted its unemployment
forecast to show a peak of 5.1% in at end-2028, the end of the
forecast horizon. This largely reflects stronger labour supply,
with forecasts for employment growth essentially unchanged.

We have repeatedly flagged that cost-of-living pressures are
likely to keep people in the labour force and thus boost the
participation rate. The RBA had previously not factored this
adequately into its forecasts but acknowledged in the SMP
that this mechanism had kept participation higher than it had
expected at the time of the August SMP.
However, because inflation surprised to the upside recently,
this additional labour supply has had no bearing on the

RBA’s assessment of capacity pressures. The RBA assesses
the domestic economy to still be a little tight, and tighter
than previously judged. The RBA’s framework for assessing
the degree of spare capacity or tightness in the economy
necessarily translates an upside surprise on inflation (or unit
labour costs) into a conclusion that the economy must be
tighter than previously judged. Indeed, the RBA’s estimate
of the NAIRU (unemployment rate consistent with inflation

stable at target) has been revised up to 4.75%.
The RBA highlighted that elevated inflation expectations may
be contributing to higher realised inflation. Consumer inflation
expectations have started to lift again but remain below where
they peaked earlier this year. However, the RBA appears
“RBA lifts cash rate 25bps to 4.85% in split
vote, citing “broadly based” domestic inflation
pressures, Middle East conflict and AI boom."

to be putting more attention on the inflation expectations
of firms and the frequency of mentions of pricing in their
liaison program. This is putting a lot of weight on proprietary
information that is not clearly validated by other, longer-
running data sources.

The AI boom is heavily cited in the SMP and other post-
meeting communication. The RBA has lifted its forecasts
for major trading partners, especially high-income Asian
economies that are the centre of high-tech manufacturing.
Demand for AI-related goods is also cited as a driver of both
global and domestic inflation. And although the RBA has
revised up its near-term forecasts for business investment
on the back of strong data centre activity, it is working on

the assumption that Australia does not become ‘a significant
regional hub for data centres’. Nor does it allow for any boost
to productivity trends from the technology, even in 2028. This
is a very different view of the risks from AI than the RBNZ
expressed last week. For the RBA, it is all about inflation, a
little bit about pressure on domestic construction resources,
and nothing about either an ugly unwind of the current
financing boom or a future productivity dividend.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: whiplash
total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of
Australia’s data-centre investment boom, which should feature
prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3
National Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term

elections. In the House, Senate and a series of State Governor
races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come
2027, the Democrats will have a comfortable majority in both
houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-
of-living pressures. The election result also limits President
Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against
Iran. Notably, both immediately before and after the election,

President Trump and the administration have exhibited a greater
interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not
a surprise that Brent oil has backed off its most recent highs
around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to
near USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since
traded back up to above USD99, still 16% below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a
robust broad-based rally in major equity indexes, concerns

over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related
infrastructure buildout jolted US equities lower overnight – the
Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of
the prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and
FOMC rate expectations have also been scaled back; so, while
tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better
outcomes across the rest of the US economy may become
evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest

their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in
the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s concerns over US inflation
should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not
hike in December is a high bar, but the probability of further
hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate
by 25bps to 4.00% at their November meeting as expected.
On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to

rise from 3.5%yr at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity
data has also surprised to the upside in recent months, though
"there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other
prices and wages".
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase
at its November meeting, taking the cash rate to 4.85%. The
move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued
concern around inflation following the recent run higher in
global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation
is now not expected to return to the mid-point of the
target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth
expectations and the unemployment rate trending up to
5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with
three members preferring to remain on hold given downside
risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth.
Governor Bullock’s suggestion that it was time to “take stock”

after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference
to raising rates further “if needed”. In our view, February
represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but
we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible.
Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in October to now
be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline
is approaching the scale of previous housing corrections but

appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in
September, but this was driven by volatile unit approvals while
detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates
and the established-market downturn are beginning to weigh
on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile
rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3; however, much of
the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly

for fuel. Real spending volumes expanded by a much more
modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at
income, spending and saving flows. The latest update suggests
that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are
increasingly being squeezed, particularly those with low savings
buffers. With another rate increase now delivered, housing
wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like

the high-water mark for growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus
widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-monetary gold trade
yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The
most notable development was another exceptionally strong
result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the Q3
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics

In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the

was a decision that needs to be made in December, but rather
a risk to be judged as additional information is received. This
points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027
than before, and only if conditions / risks warrant.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
No deals are done
This week’s developments underscore our message that
a rate hike at the RBNZ’s December meeting is far from a
done deal. New Zealand’s labour market remains softer
than expected, and that will be noted by those on the

policy committee who question the extent of second-round
inflation pressures. The rout in the US mid-term elections
has also taken significant heat out of oil prices for now,
although it’s unclear whether that situation will hold through
to next month. Our view on the endpoint for this OCR cycle
remains unchanged, but there’s a growing risk that the RBNZ
could slow the pace of tightening in the new year.
The September quarter labour market surveys pointed to

ongoing softness in the jobs market. The unemployment
rate rose from 5.6% to 5.7%, continuing a four-year run
of increases, and reaching its highest level since 2015.
Employment was softer than expected, and wage growth
remains subdued despite increasing cost-of-living pressures.
The Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS) showed a surprise
0.1% fall in employment in the September quarter, after a
downwardly revised 0.3% increase in the June quarter. That

went against the Monthly Employment Indicator (MEI) measure
of filled jobs, which had suggested some upward momentum
emerging in the jobs market (and notably, the HLFS measure
had outpaced the MEI for the previous nine straight quarters).
The fall in HLFS employment was accompanied by a fall in
the labour force participation rate from 70.6% to 70.4%.
These co-movements essentially reversed the surprisingly
large increases that we saw in the June quarter, which we had

suggested at the time were more likely due to sampling error
in the survey rather than a genuine surge.
We generally recommend focusing on the unemployment rate,
which is less vulnerable to sampling errors – and the message
here is one of softness. Our best interpretation is that, to the
extent that jobs are growing, it’s not fast enough to absorb the
growth in the population (which itself is a fairly low bar, as net
migration remains at a low ebb).

Even with the economy returning to growth over the last
year or so, hiring activity has remained weak. This may be
an ongoing legacy of employment trends in previous years.
Businesses have told us that they felt burned by the severe
skill shortages that developed over 2021-23, and looked to
hold on to workers to the extent they could during the 2024-
25 downturn. The flipside of this is that they may now find
themselves overstaffed even as activity picks up again.

This shows up most strikingly in labour force transition rates.
The rise in unemployment over the last few years has not
been driven by an increase in job losses, but by accumulation
of people who, once out of work, are finding it increasingly
difficult to get back in again. That also extends to young
people trying to enter the workforce for the first time – youth
unemployment of around 25% is the highest in the history of
the survey going back to 1986.

The Labour Cost Index (LCI) rose by 0.5% in the September
quarter, in line with our expectations overall but a bit weaker
in the details – private sector wages were up by 0.5%, while
there was a surprisingly large 0.7% increase in public sector
wages. There was a notable jump in the number of employers
citing cost-of-living adjustments as a reason for increasing pay
rates – although the size of those increases varied a great deal,
with many substantially less than the headline inflation rate.

Potential RBNZ implications
The labour market data was unequivocally weaker than
expected by either us or the RBNZ. While likely not a
gamechanger for the cycle overall, it will add ammunition to the
arguments of the doves on the Monetary Policy Committee that
a pause in interest rate rises will make sense soon – perhaps in
December or early next year. It’s likely the case that interest rate
markets were over-pricing the odds of a hike at the 9 December

MPS, so the subsequent pullback in pricing makes sense.
The message from the RBNZ in the October meeting was to
be careful about pricing in "done deals", and that the outlook
Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
will be data-dependent. As we noted at the time, alongside
measures of inflation expectations, the key domestic data
to watch ahead of the RBNZ’s 9 December meeting will
be indicators of what is happening to excess capacity (the
output gap), as this will be a key determinant of the Bank’s

assessment of the outlook for inflation over the coming year.
The RBNZ looks at a broad suite of indicators of labour market
slack, although their research found that only four of them
seem to have predictive power: the unemployment rate,
the vacancy to unemployment ratio, the QSBO measure of
labour as the main limiting factor to growth, and the rate of
transition between jobs. The first three of these all pointed
to a softening in the September quarter. (The fourth one is

calculated from income tax data, so it’s not publicly available
and even the RBNZ receives it only with a lag.)
In the eyes of the dovish MPC members, the softness in
the labour market indicators will likely push in the direction
of having greater comfort with the medium-term inflation
outlook. The outcome of the election, with the possibility of a
return to a dual inflation/employment mandate, could increase
the prominence of these factors. This likely won’t be an explicit

factor in the RBNZ’s reasoning at its December review, but it
may be in the minds of individual MPC members.
That said, it’s important to remember that in the RBNZ’s
forecasting framework, the GDP figures are the main
determinant of excess capacity in the economy. The most
recent GDP data tells us that the output gap probably
narrowed a bit through the June quarter, mainly reflecting
historical revisions. In light of the latest labour market figures,

there may be some downside risk to the outlook for Q3 GDP –
but there are conflicting indicators there, so it’s not that clear.
At last month’s surprise on-hold OCR decision, Governor
Breman lamented that there was no protocol for the RBNZ
to be able to signal how it was reading the economic data –
in particular the softer than expected Q3 CPI print – ahead
of the policy meeting. In that light, it will be interesting to
see whether they use some already-scheduled speaking

engagements to signal what they make of the labour market
data. Top of mind is Chief Economist Conway's 10 November
speech to the UBS Australasia Conference (pointedly titled
“How is the NZ economy tracking”). Governor Breman is also
speaking to the Institute of Directors on 19 November.
Our view remains that the December OCR hike is more likely
than not. But there will be a clear debate to be had between
the MPC hawks and doves, and it may again come down to a

vote. We still expect a 4% peak in the OCR for this cycle, but
the time taken to get there could be more flexible. Takes along
the lines of "3% is the terminal rate" seem rather hasty on the
basis of a single data release.
Aside from how the Committee interprets the labour market
data, the key considerations in December will be: the RBNZ’s
surveys of inflation expectations by households and businesses;
whether the US mid-term elections lead to a sustained de-

escalation in the Middle East and a pullback in oil prices; and
whether developments in the AI space exacerbate some MPC
members’ concerns about the global economic environment.
And then of course there’s the result of the General Election,
with voting closing on Saturday evening. The actual formation
of a government is likely to take weeks of negotiation, so the
outcome may not be known by the 9 December MPS but at least
the range of possibilities should be clearer. We’ll publish our

initial thoughts on the election results early on Monday morning.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Oct Retail Card Spending (%mth)
Nov 12, Last: –0.4, Westpac f/c: 1.4
We’re forecasting a 1.4% rise in retail spending in October.
While that would be a solid increase, it’s in large part due to
the 11% rise in petrol prices over the past month. Stripping out

fuel spending, we’re forecasting a 0.6% rise in core spending
categories. Core spending has been very choppy recently,
and the increase we’re assuming follows falls in the past few
months. A result in line with our expectations would leave the
trend in core spending relatively flat since the start of this year
with the rise in fuel prices weighing on spending in other areas.
AUS: Nov Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Nov 10, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment fell 4.7% to 80.4 in Oct, returning to the

deeply pessimistic levels seen earlier in the year. Responses
over the course of the survey week showed a very sharp
deterioration after the RBA rate hike.
The Nov survey is in the field over the week ended Nov 7.
Interest rates will again factor with the RBA raising the cash
rate by another 25bps to 4.85%, an 18yr high. Average pump
prices also pushed higher, the national average just under
$2.40/litre, while the housing market downturn has continued

to broaden and deepen. The US mid-term election results may
have some effects on sentiment locally as well.
AUS: Q3 Housing Finance Approvals (%qtr)
Nov 11 Last: –5.2, Westpac f/c: –10
Market f/c: –8.5, Range: –15 to –5
The total value of housing finance approvals fell The value of
new dwelling finance approvals excl. refinance fell 5.2%qtr,
the largest quarterly drop in three years. Annual growth eased
to 7.3%yr. The weakness was accounted for mainly by lower

lending volumes: loan numbers were down 4.0%qtr (–0.6%yr),
while average loan size also contributed negatively.
Q3 is expected to see an even bigger decline with the full
impact of interest rate rises and tax policy changes showing
through. The value of both loan enquiries and turnover fell by
around 10% in the quarter. We expect total finance approvals
to show a 10% drop as well, with investor loan approvals likely
to be down 16%qtr and owner occupier approvals down 6%qtr.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
US: Oct CPI (%mth)
Nov 11, Headline, Last: 0.6, Market f/c: 0.3, Westpac f/c: 0.4
US consumer prices rose by 0.6% in September, although
half of the gain was due to energy inflation, with core prices
instead up 0.3%. Over the year, prices were reported to have

risen 3.7%yr overall and 2.6%yr excluding food and energy.
Constructively, ex food, energy and shelter, annual inflation
met the FOMC’s medium-term target of 2.0%yr.
Come October, energy price pressures are set to recede
given the pre-election run higher in oil prices came late in the
month. A softer print for shelter is also likely to moderate core
momentum. We see a 0.4% gain for the headline measure and
a 0.2% result for core inflation as most probable. Looking to

2027, disinflation is expected to continue at a measured pace
towards the medium-term target.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN DWELLING

Units lift the headline
2 November 2026
Key points
• Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth
in September, although the annual pace
moved into contraction at –0.8%yr with
the detail suggesting the underlying
cycle is starting to turn down.

• The headline increase was driven by
another volatile rise in private unit
approvals (+16.6%) which more than
offset a significant decline in private
detached house approvals (–5.3%).
• Units drove gains in NSW and
Queensland. WA, SA and Victoria all
recorded declines. Detached dwelling
approvals fell across all states.
• The total value of residential approvals
fell 2.8%mth but a sharp rise in non-
residential approvals – led by the
data centres – saw the total value of

building approvals rise 13%mth.
• Interest rate increases, weakness in
established housing markets, rising costs
and pessimistic consumer sentiment
are likely to remain headwinds.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth in September, while
the annual pace moved into contraction at –0.8%yr. The
result was well above both Westpac’s expectation for a
7.0%mth decline and the market expectation for a 2.0%mth
rise. Unit approvals continued to drive headline volatility,

surging in September, while detached dwellings, which
provide a better read on the underlying trend, recorded a
sizeable decline.
Note that most figures in the charts and table are presented on
a rolling-three month basis to smooth out monthly volatility.
Private detached dwelling approvals fell 5.3%mth in
September, unwinding August’s upwardly-revised 5.4%mth
rise (+1.7ppts). Recent resilience has kept the segment
elevated, with approvals remaining above 10,000 and the

annual pace at 9.6%yr. However, the quarterly profile provides
a clearer read on the underlying trend and suggests detached
dwelling approvals are beginning to respond to recent interest
rate increases (see here). Approvals rose just 0.9%qtr in
September vs 3.1%qtr in June and 5.9%qtr in March.
Private unit approvals remained volatile, rising 16.6%mth in
September following 21.7%mth decline in August. Strength
was led by high-rise approvals, while low-mid-rise approvals

recorded a more modest increase. Despite the monthly rise,
the annual pace fell further into contraction at –20.5%yr.
Among the states, NSW recorded the strongest result,
with private approvals rising 35.3%mth led by a surge in
private unit approvals. Queensland also recorded an increase
(+9.3%mth), while WA (–2.9%mth), SA (–3.5%mth) and
Victoria (–29.0%mth) all declined.
In value terms, residential building approvals fell 2.8%mth,
driven by a 3.3%mth decline in new residential approvals,

partially offset by a 0.8%mth rise in renovation activity.
Non-residential approvals once again reversed sharply, rising
45.9%mth, as the data centre-dominated ‘other commercial’
segment recorded an 8-fold increase on a non-seasonally
adjusted basis. The total value of building approvals rose
13.0%mth in September. On a non-seasonally adjusted basis,
the value of building approvals rose 8.3%mth overall, but
declined 3.0%mth when excluding the ‘other commercial’

segment.
September’s result reinforces the importance of looking
through headline volatility in unit approvals. While total
approvals rose in the month, detached dwelling approvals
slowed on a quarterly basis and fell across all states, pointing
to softer underlying momentum. The annual pace of total
approvals also moved into contraction for the first time
since January. Interest rate increases, the sharp downturn
in established housing markets and rising costs are likely to

weigh on further growth, with pessimistic consumer sentiment
an additional headwind (see here).
Detached approvals point to softer momentum

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -32.53 điểm tương ứng -1.83%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Linh kiện ô tô, Dầu khí và Công nghệ là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt nhất 
trong tuần. Khối ngoại duy trì bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị -634 tỷ 
đồng, tập trung ở các mã FPT, ACB và VPL; mặt khác, tự doanh đảo chiều bán ròng ở mức 
-609 tỷ đồng.

qua và đang duy trì vị thế bán lũy kế -1,462 hợp đồng. Tự doanh duy trì mua ròng 722 hợp 
đồng nhưng đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -5,278 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam đánh mất đà hồi phục của tuần trước, khi VN-Index quay đầu giảm khoảng 
1,8% về đóng cửa quanh 1.742 điểm, với phiên cuối tuần có thời điểm lùi sâu về sát 1.727 
điểm trước khi thu hẹp đà giảm. Dù mùa báo cáo KQKD quý 3 diễn ra tương đối tích cực, 
tuy nhiên các lo ngại về việc môi trường lãi suất cao vẫn duy trì trong bối cảnh căng thẳng 
Trung Đông leo thang đang đè nặng lên tâm lý nhà đầu tư. Kết quả kỳ họp của FED trong 
tuần qua nhìn chung phù hợp với kỳ vọng và không gây ra ảnh hưởng đáng kể. Điểm kém 
tích cực nhất là khối ngoại đã quay lại bán ròng với tổng giá trị khoảng 2.700 tỷ đồng 
trong tuần — đảo ngược diễn biến mua ròng cuối tuần trước, cho thấy đà rút vốn ngoại 
vẫn chưa thực sự dừng lại.

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn (Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh 
Xanh nhạt  Khả năng tăng điểm vừa phải Mua từng phần 
Vàng  Xu hướng trung tính Mua/Bán trading 
Cam  Khả năng giảm điểm vừa phải Bán từng phần 
Đỏ  Khả năng giảm điểm cao Bán mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX tuần qua hình thành mẫu nến giảm trên đồ thị tuần, nhưng chưa thoái lui quá 1 
nửa cây nến tăng của tuần trước, cùng thanh khoản được tiết giảm, hàm ý nhịp điều chỉnh 
vẫn trong phạm vi cho phép. Trên khung ngày, sau bốn phiên giằng co đầu tuần với thanh 
khoản thấp, chỉ số đã chịu áp lực sụt giảm mạnh hơn trong phiên cuối tuần, chớm xuống 
dưới đường tín hiệu chính giữa dải Bollinger, nhưng cho tín hiệu rút chân ngay sau đó, thể 
hiện áp lực bán tháo đã tạm thời được kiểm soát. Mặc dù rủi ro mở rộng thêm nhịp điều 
chỉnh vẫn còn để ngỏ, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn đang được bảo lưu, VNINDEX 
được kỳ vọng sẽ cho phản ứng hồi phục trở lại tại các điểm đỡ bên dưới.

(Ghi chú: NĐT có thể dựa theo nhận định xu hướng thị trường để tham khảo khuyến nghị 
hành động tương ứng trong bảng phía trên) 
Kháng cự xa: 1850 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1800 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1720 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1680 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

Document 293: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-06
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research – BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

Document 297: Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-14)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ whiplash (2026-11-14).pdf

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: whiplash
By Ryan Wells & Elliot Clarke
11:20 November 06 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the RBA delivered another 25bp rate increase at its November meeting, taking the cash
rate to 4.85%. The move was widely expected, reflecting the Board’s continued concern around
inflation following the recent run higher in global energy prices, firmer domestic capacity pressures and

signs that inflation expectations have edged up. Inflation is now not expected to return to the mid-point
of the target band until mid-2028. This is despite weaker growth expectations and the unemployment

### Page 2

rate trending up to 5.1% by end-2028 on the RBA’s forecasts.
Importantly, though, the decision was not unanimous, with three members preferring to remain on hold
given downside risks to activity and the restraining influence of slower growth. Governor Bullock’s
suggestion that it was time to “take stock” after five rate increases also struck a less hawkish note,
while the statement dropped September’s explicit reference to raising rates further “if needed”. In our

view, February represents the next plausible opportunity for another hike, but we see this only as a risk.
Policy’s effect on housing is becoming increasingly visible. Cotality home prices fell a further 1.2% in
October to now be 6.7% below March’s peak. The cumulative price decline is approaching the scale of
previous housing corrections but appears to have regained momentum after September’s rate
increase. Dwelling approvals saw a 5.1% headline gain in September, but this was driven by volatile

unit approvals while detached house approvals fell sharply, suggesting higher rates and the
established-market downturn are beginning to weigh on the construction pipeline.
Official data on nominal household spending meanwhile rose 0.6% in September and 2.1% for Q3;
however, much of the recent acceleration looks to be price-driven, particularly for fuel. Real spending
volumes expanded by a much more modest (but still solid) 0.7% in Q3.
The Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel provides a granular look at income, spending and saving flows.

The latest update suggests that, beneath the resilient headline story, households are increasingly being
squeezed, particularly those with low savings buffers. With another rate increase now delivered,
housing wealth declining and sentiment weak, Q3 increasingly looks like the high-water mark for
growth in consumer spending.
Lastly for Australia, in September the goods trade surplus widened sharply to $2.8bn, with non-
monetary gold trade yet again adding considerable noise month-to-month. The most notable

development was another exceptionally strong result for data-processing equipment imports, taking the
Q3 total to $11.1bn. This is another clear marker of the scale of Australia’s data-centre investment
boom, which should feature prominently in both business investment and imports in the Q3 National
Accounts.
Offshore, the world has been focused on the US mid-term elections. In the House, Senate and a series
of State Governor races, the Democrats experienced resounding victory. Come 2027, the Democrats

will have a comfortable majority in both houses of Congress and are expected to make use of this
strength to respond to voter concerns, particularly over cost-of-living pressures. The election result also
limits President Trump’s ability to fund an extended military action against Iran. Notably, both
immediately before and after the election, President Trump and the administration have exhibited a
greater interest in furthering negotiations with Iran. It is therefore not a surprise that Brent oil has

backed off its most recent highs around USD118 last Friday, although the scale of the drop to near
USD93 yesterday is arguably too optimistic. Brent has since traded back up to above USD99, still 16%
below Friday’s peak.
While the initial reaction to the US election result was a robust broad-based rally in major equity
indexes, concerns over the potential for tighter regulation of AI and the related infrastructure buildout

### Page 3

Related articles
jolted US equities lower overnight – the Nasdaq falling 5.5% and S&P500 4.4%, both a multiple of the
prior session’s gains. That said, US term interest rates and FOMC rate expectations have also been
scaled back; so, while tech may come under greater scrutiny, a foundation for better outcomes across
the rest of the US economy may become evident in time. US data out this week continued to suggest
their economy is in robust health overall. And, if tensions in the Middle East abate, the FOMC’s

concerns over US inflation should subside. Gaining enough comfort for the FOMC to not hike in
December is a high bar, but the probability of further hikes in 2027 should continue to fall.
Turning then to the UK, the Bank of England raised Bank Rate by 25bps to 4.00% at their November
meeting as expected. On the Bank's baseline expectation, UK inflation is forecast to rise from 3.5%yr
at September to 5.00% by mid-2027. Activity data has also surprised to the upside in recent months,

though "there remains limited evidence of knock-on effects on other prices and wages".
In the press conference, Governor Bailey recognised the nascent improvement in energy price
pressures over the past 48 hours and the effectiveness of monetary policy but also conveyed that
"Unless progress towards resolving the conflict is made, or there is other news which softens the risk to
inflationary pressures, a further increase in Bank Rate may be warranted". Governor Bailey's tone and

comments made in the minutes by other Committee members did not suggest this was a decision that
needs to be made in December, but rather a risk to be judged as additional information is received.
This points to further tightening being more likely in early 2027 than before, and only if conditions / risks
warrant.

2

STRONG RESILIENCE TO EXTERNAL SHOCKS
, 
SOLID 
MACROECONOMIC FUNDAMENTALS 
◼ 
 Over the past 
 two years
 , India has demonstrated remarkable resilience in the face of external shocks, while its 
macroeconomic fundamentals have remained solid.  
◼ 
 In FY2025/2026  
(ended in March 2026)
 , India was hit by a sharp rise in US tariffs. However, thanks to supportive fiscal 
and monetary policies, 
 real GDP  
grew by 7.8% in FY2026, placing India among the fastest
 -
 growing Asian economies.  
◼ 
 For the current fiscal year, the Indian economy is facing rising energy prices 
 (
due to 
 the conflict in the Middle East and 
increased food prices 
 because 
 of 
an 
unfavourable monsoon
 ) 
as well as 
 monetary tightening. Nevertheless, despite these 
new challenges, real GDP growth is expected to exceed 7%.  
◼ 
 India is currently in a stronger position than it was in 2022 to cope with the new energy shock. The fiscal leeway to support  
the economy has increased with the decline in the fiscal deficit, and inflation is more contained.  
◼ 
 Although 
 rising  
energy prices have put pressure on external accounts, FX reserves 
 remain  
sufficient to cover the country’s 
short
 -
 term external financing needs. 
 Yet, 
downward pressures on the rupee have been 
 significant 
 and 
could persist in the 
short term
 . 
In 
H1 
2026, India’s external accounts withstood the rise in energy prices fairly well
 , notably  
thanks to the sharp increase 
in workers’ remittances. 
 T
he 
widening of the trade deficit was primarily due to the substantial increase in machinery and 
equipment imports 
 while t
 he oil trade deficit increased 
 only 
moderately, as the rise in energy prices was offset by a decline 
in import volumes. 
 However, 
in H2 2026, the oil trade deficit is expected to widen significantly.  
Additionally, large portfolio outflows have exacerbated downward pressure on the rupee, necessitating interventions by 
the central bank in foreign exchange markets and the adoption of regulatory measures to support the currency. Although 
these measures hav
 e led to a rebound in the rupee (at least temporarily), inflationary pressures, volatile portfolio 
investments, and low net foreign direct investments are 
 expected to continue 
 to put pressure on the currency.  
◼ 
 D
ownward pressures on the rupee 
 and 
 rising bond yields will reduce the government's 
 already limited 
 fiscal capacity to 
increase public investment and 
 support economic development. However, this will not undermine its ability to refinance 
its debt, given its safe structure.  
◼ 
 T
he banking sector is sufficiently robust to cope with the expected rise in credit risks.

COUNTRY RISK PROFILE

Non transfer risk   
India’s external accounts are solid with a low external debt, moderate current account deficit and large FX 
reserves. 
 India’s net debtor position reached only 5.9% of GDP in Q2 2026. In the short term, external accounts 
are expected to deteriorate 
 given

high.

Systemic credit risk  
The banking sector has strengthened over the past five years
 , 
as illustrated by the improvement in 
 asset  
quality 
and capital buffers. The main sources of credit risks are rising household debt, the rise in energy prices, the 
unfavourable monsoon and monetary tightening.  
◼ 
 The quality of assets has improved significantly. 
 In Q1 2026, the banking sector’s non
 -
 performing loan (NPL) ratio fell 
to a multi
 -
 decade low of 1.8%. For public banks  
(
54.4% of total loans
 )
, the NPL ratio declined to 2.1%.  
◼ 
 Capital buffers are strong. 
 While 
loan loss provisions remained modest at 75.6% of NPLs, capital buffers stayed strong. 
The Capital Adequacy Ratio (CAR) rose to 17.7% in Q1 2026, up from 17.4% a year earlier. From April 2027, Indian banks 
will adopt a new Expected Credit Loss (ECL) framewo
 rk, requiring earlier provisioning for potential loan losses. In the 
short term, higher provisioning requirements could push up lending rates and tighten credit conditions 
 while 
 triggering 
capital raising or targeted government recapitalisation. 
 I
n the medium term, the reform could strengthen banking  
sector 
resilience through earlier recognition of credit risks and more robust loss buffers.

Document 326: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.
